[result: #!/bin/bash
# PostToolUse hook: run only the test module that covers the edited file.
# Input is the hook JSON on stdin; the edited path is tool_input.file_path.
set -u
cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"
# Worktrees share the primary checkout's virtual environment.
MAIN="$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)/.."
FILE=$(python3 -c 'import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin).get("tool_input",{}).get("file_path",""))')
case "$FILE" in
  */helpdesk/tests/test_*.py) MOD="helpdesk.tests.$(basename "${FILE%.py}")" ;;
  */helpdesk/*.py)
    NAME=$(basename "${FILE%.py}")
    [ -f "helpdesk/tests/test_]

[result: 1	# Django 4.2 -> 5.2 migration units
2	
3	Split of the work described in `docs/migration/inventory.md` into units that can be
4	done by one subagent each, in any order, without two units touching the same file.
5	
6	## Ground rules for every unit
7	
8	- Two environments. `.venv` holds Django 4.2.30 and is the baseline; `.venv52`
9	  holds Django 5.2.17.
10	
11	- Every unit is done when **both** commands are green:
12	
13	  ```bash
14	  .venv52/bin/python quicktest.py <the unit's test modules>   # target
15	  .venv/bin/python quicktest.py <the unit's test modules>     # baseline, still 4.2
16	]

Baseline: Django 4.2.30 installed in `.venv`. `constraints-Django5.txt` already pins
`Django >=5,<6`, so the target is 5.2 LTS.

This document is an inventory only. It lists, per module, the framework surfaces the
code touches, the tests that exercise those files, and a risk rating.

Risk ratings mean:

- **High** - a known removal or behaviour change in 5.0/5.1/5.2 hits this code, or a
  large surface is exercised with thin tests.
- **Medium** - the module uses framework surfaces that changed, but the specific calls
  look compatible, or coverage is go]

[result: 1	# Django 4.2 -> 5.2 migration units
2	
3	Split of the work described in `docs/migration/inventory.md` into units that can be
4	done by one subagent each, in any order, without two units touching the same file.
5	
6	## Ground rules for every unit
7	
8	- Two environments. `.venv` holds Django 4.2.30 and is the baseline; `.venv52`
9	  holds Django 5.2.17.
10	
11	- Every unit is done when **both** commands are green:
12	
13	  ```bash
14	  .venv52/bin/python quicktest.py <the unit's test modules>   # target
15	  .venv/bin/python quicktest.py <the unit's test modules>     # baseline, still 4.2
16	]

# Utilities


def localtime(value=None, timezone=None):
    """
    Convert an aware datetime.datetime to local time.

    Only aware datetimes are allowed. When value is omitted, it defaults to
    now().

    Local time is defined by the current time zone, unless another time zone
    is specified.
    """
    if value is None:
        value = now()
    if timezone is None:
        timezone = get_current_timezone()
    # Emulate the behavior of astimezone() on Python < 3]

[result: """
Tests for how ``helpdesk/templates/helpdesk`` renders forms.

Django 5.0 changed the default form renderer from ``django/forms/table.html`` to
``django/forms/div.html``. Nothing in the suite asserted on rendered form markup, so
that change could land silently. These tests assert on what a template guarantees -
which label goes with which field id, where errors sit, which element wraps what - so
they hold on Django 4.2 and 5.2 alike, and fail if a template ever falls back to
whichever renderer Django happens to default to.
"""

2	"""Failure injection for the email ingestion path.

6	assumes, an attachment whose filename the filesystem cannot take, or a mailbox
7	connection that dies half way through a fetch. Every test here asserts how
8	`process_email` / `extract_email_metadata` degrade under one of those, so a
9	future change that turns a logged failure into a silent message drop fails loudly.

Author: Waseem Khan <ceo@wazeem.com>
Date:   Wed Sep 2 15:28:36 2026 +0500

    Unit 2: keep helpdesk's own anonymous-access policy under Django 5.1+
    
    Django 5.1 added LoginRequiredMiddleware. It redirects every anonymous
    request unless the resolved view carries login_required = False, and it
    runs in process_view - before helpdesk's own gate. A host project that
    turns it on therefore never reaches protect_view, which silently turns
    HELPDESK_ANON_ACCESS_RAISES_404, HELPDESK_REDIRECT_TO_LOGIN_BY_DEFAULT
    and HELPDESK_PUBL]

class TestPublicViewsOptOutOfBlanketLoginRequirement(TestCase):
    """
    Django 5.1's LoginRequiredMiddleware redirects every anonymous request
    unless the resolved view carries ``login_required = False``. helpdesk has
    its own anonymous-access policy (HELPDESK_ANON_ACCESS_RAISES_404,
    HELPDESK_REDIRECT_TO_LOGIN_BY_DEFAULT, HELPDESK_PUBLIC_VIEW_PROTECTOR), so
    the views that policy governs must opt out or the settings go dead.
    """

    PUBLIC_VIEW_NAMES = [

# PostToolUse hook: run only the test module that covers the edited file.
# Input is the hook JSON on stdin; the edited path is tool_input.file_path.
set -u
cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"
# Worktrees share the primary checkout's virtual environment.

FILE=$(python3 -c 'import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin).get("tool_input",{}).get("file_path",""))')
case "$FILE" in
  */helpdesk/tests/test_*.py) MOD="helpdesk.tests.$(basename "${FILE%.py}")" ;;
  */helpdesk/*.py)
    NAME=$(basename "${FILE%.py}")
    [ -f "helpdesk/tests/test_]

# Django 5.0 gave forms.URLField an "assume_scheme" argument for the scheme it
# prepends to a schemeless value. Leaving it unset raises RemovedInDjango60Warning
# on 5.x and lets the assumed scheme change from "http" to "https" in Django 6.0
# (django/forms/fields.py:778 in 5.2.17), which would silently rewrite what a "url"
# custom field stores. Pin the 4.2 behaviour on both versions; 4.2's URLField does
# not take the argument, hence the version guard rather than an unconditional kwarg.
URLFIELD_ASSUME_SCHEME_KWARGS = (
    {"assume_scheme": "http"} if django.VERSION ]

commit f3e6982d3276ad3bf4f3b228c4067dda64341538
Author: Waseem Khan <ceo@wazeem.com>
Date:   Wed Sep 2 16:43:19 2026 +0500

    Unit 9: pin the url custom field's assumed scheme, cover the custom field path
    
    Django 5.0 added forms.URLField(assume_scheme=...). Leaving it unset raises
    RemovedInDjango60Warning on 5.x and lets the assumed scheme flip from "http"

Full suite as a regression check: 4.2 `Ran 383 tests ... OK (skipped=5)`; 5.2 still only the 3 pre-existing `test_api` failures (Unit 6's, a DRF `OrderedDict` vs `dict` repr, unrelated to my change).

## 3. Headline findings

**One source change was forced**, in `helpdesk/forms.py` (new constant at :51-58, new branch at :111-112): `CUSTOMFIELD_TO_FIELD_DICT["url"]` is `forms.URLField`, and Django 5.0 added `assume_scheme` (`django/forms/fields.py:778` in the installed 5.2.17). Leaving it unset raises `RemovedInDjango60Warning` on every instantiation, and the assumed scheme becomes `https` in 6.0, which would silently change what `TicketCustomFieldValue` stores for a submitter typing `example.com`. Now `{"assume_scheme": "http"} if django.VERSION >= (5, 0) else {}`, so both versions clean to `http://example.com`. Evidence: same probe under both interpreters showed identical `clean()` output and the warning only on 5.2; after the change the warning line is gone from the diff.

**Markup search: nothing to change.** Neither owned module has a `<p>`/`<table>` assertion. `test_checklist` has seven plain-text `assertContains`; `test_ticket_submission` has one plain-text one plus three widget-markup assertions in `test_ticket_field_autofill` (`select_option.html`/`input.html`, byte-identical between the two installed trees). They are nearer the edge than they look: 5.0 adds `aria-describedby`/`aria-invalid` and an error-list id, so they survive only because they stop at `value="..."`. Probed and quoted in the report.

**Checked and unchanged** (all diffed against the installed 5.2 source, then probed): every other field class; `Media.merge` (rewritten onto `graphlib.TopologicalSorter`, byte-identical output for the two `Media` classes here plus shared-tail, three-way, conflicting-order and css merges); ModelForm field ordering for all 13 `ModelForm`s despite the 5.x `form_declared_fields` change; `deletion_widget`; `ChoiceField.choices` (now normalised to 2-tuples, same values/labels/order); `validate_file_extension` (identical accept/reject on both).

**New coverage:** 63 tests. Every `data_type` round-tripped through `_add_form_custom_fields` -> `save()` -> `TicketCustomFieldValue.value` -> back out as `EditTicketForm`'s initial; required-empty and invalid-value rejections asserting no ticket and no value row; the staff_only filter both ways; the validator matrix and the public attachment field end to end (`.exe` rejected through a real POST, `.txt` reaching a `FollowUpAttachment` — the path Unit 8 unblocked); media order; checklist forms; `MultipleTicketSelectForm`'s three unexercised rejection branches.

**Pinned, not fixed:** `ChecklistTemplateForm.clean_task_list` (forms.py:768-770) maps `strip` over whatever JSON it gets, so a JSON string explodes into characters and a JSON number raises `TypeError` out of the form and becomes a 500. Both pinned with tests naming the bug; intended fix in the report.

## 4. Left alone for other units

- `quicktest.py` (no unit owns it) does not set `USE_TZ`, and Django 5.0 flipped the default to True — so the 4.2 and 5.2 verification runs are not the same configuration. Both project settings files already set `USE_TZ = True`; the integrator should decide whether the suite pins it.
- `helpdesk/tests/test_checklist.py:170` passes a naive `datetime(2023, 5, 1)`, which makes 5.2 emit `RuntimeWarning: ... received a naive datetime while time zone support is active`. Existing test code, so left as is; the exact edit (`completion_date=timezone.now()`, correct on both, unlike `make_aware`) is in the report.
- `helpdesk/models.py:2069` `_choices_as_array` returns lists of lists (Unit 8) — informational only, nothing to fix.
- `helpdesk/forms.py:248` `EditTicketCustomFieldForm.save()` misses its `return` and so breaks the `ModelForm.save()` contract. Harmless today (`update_ticket.py:322` discards the result), deliberately not pinned so a future one-line fix needs no test change.

import pathlib

from django.core.exceptions import SuspiciousFileOperation


def validate_file_name(name, allow_relative_path=False):
    # Remove potentially dangerous names
    if os.path.basename(name) in {"", ".", ".."}:
        raise SuspiciousFileOperation("Could not derive file name from '%s'" % name)

    if allow_relative_path:
        # Ensure that name can be treated as a pure posix path, i.e. Unix
        # style (with forward slashes).
        path = pathlib.PurePosixPath(str(name).replace("\\", "/"))
        if path.is_absolute() or ".." in path.parts:
            raise]

10 tests to 72. The 4 skips are the version-gated LoginRequiredMiddleware end-to-end assertions; each has a non-gated sibling asserting the same guarantee on 4.2 via the attribute the middleware reads. Full-suite regression: 4.2 is 382 OK; 5.2 is 382 with exactly the 3 pre-existing `test_api` failures (Unit 6), unchanged from baseline.

## 3. Headline findings

**One thing the migration forced, and it is not what the brief predicted.** The iframe views' `csrf_exempt` + `xframe_options_exempt` combination is fine on 5.2 — I read `View.as_view` (byte-identical file), `CsrfViewMiddleware.process_view`, and both decorators (rewritten for async in 5.x, but they still set the same attributes in the same places, and `csrf_exempt` still applies `wraps` last so the flag survives). What was actually broken is Django 5.1's `LoginRequiredMiddleware`. Unit 2 opted `protect_view` out, covering the five entry points wired through it. **Six more public URLs are wired into the URLconf bare, and all six are in my files**: `helpdesk:submit`, `public_change_language`, `kb_index`, `kb_category`, `kb_category_iframe`, `kb_vote`. Five now carry `@login_not_required` (Unit 2's guarded import, so identical on 4.2). `create_ticket` still runs `protect_view` internally, so the exemption hands the decision back to helpdesk's own settings rather than removing it. `kb.vote` is deliberately left out — it writes, and the right answer there is a login; a test asserts that and a negative control proves the middleware really does take it away.

**Five pre-existing bugs pinned, not fixed** (all identical on 4.2.30 and 5.2.17, verified by probe):
- `public.py:152-154` — a spam submission through the iframe view makes `form_valid` return `None` and `xframe_options_exempt` dereference it. Crash.
- `abstract_views.py:9-13` vs `:27-28` — the by-slug queue lookup is overwritten by the raw query-string value, so `?queue=<slug>` preselects nothing and the lookup is dead code. It only works because the KB templates link with the pk.
- `kb.py:49-52` — `except TypeError` misses `ValueError`, so `/kb/<slug>/?kbitem=abc` is a 500 on a public page.
- `kb.py:83-103` — an anonymous visitor can vote a KB item up without limit; `voted_by.add(None)` is silently swallowed by `bulk_create(ignore_conflicts=True)` while the counters still move.
- `public.py:231-232` / `:249-250` — the "Missing/Invalid ticket ID" messages are passed positionally to `SearchForTicketView.as_view()`, land in `self.args`, and never reach `get_context_data`. Both messages have always been dropped.

Also noted: `USE_TZ` default flipped to `True` in 5.0 and `quicktest.py` does not set it, so rendered timestamps differ between the two runs. Both shipped settings files already set it, so this is a harness artefact only.

## 4. Left alone (other units)

- `helpdesk/tests/helpers.py` (owned by nobody): `HAS_LOGIN_REQUIRED_MIDDLEWARE` / `LOGIN_REQUIRED_MIDDLEWARE` are now defined in three test modules and the "install the middleware, one request, fresh Client" helper is written twice. Both belong there; I could not extract them. Written up as item 6 of the report.
- The three `test_api` failures on 5.2 (Unit 6).
- No `DEFAULT_FILE_STORAGE` problem surfaced — Unit 8 has already landed.

## 3. Headline finding

**One source change, `helpdesk/email.py:778-793`.** `add_file_if_always_save_incoming_email_message` stamped the saved `.eml` filename with a bare `timezone.localtime()`, which raises `ValueError` on the naive datetime `now()` returns when `USE_TZ` is off. Django 5.0 flipped the `USE_TZ` default from False to True (`global_settings.py:44` in both trees), and `quicktest.py` never sets it, so the same line takes a different branch on each version. The break runs backwards from the usual case: the feature was dead on 4.2's default and the 5.0 flip hides it, but it is still broken on 5.2 for any project setting `USE_TZ = False`. Fix is `received = timezone.now()` then convert only `if timezone.is_aware(received)`.

Evidence, from reverting the guard and rerunning: 4 errors on 4.2 (including the end-to-end ingest test), 2 on 5.2 (only the tests that pin `USE_TZ = False` explicitly).

**Nothing else needed changing**, and the evidence is a probe of 40 calls run under both venvs and diffed: after the fix the only differing lines are Django facts themselves (version, `USE_TZ`, `now()` tzinfo, whether bare `localtime()` works, whether `timezone.utc` exists). Every helpdesk function returns identically. Per-API: `SimpleUploadedFile` byte-identical; `ValidationError` class body identical; `smart_str` changed only in `DjangoUnicodeDecodeError`'s internals, unreferenced here and every handler catches an unchanged base class; `BaseCommand` changed three ways, none reachable from `get_email.py`; `Q` additive only. `django.utils.timezone.utc` is confirmed gone in 5.2 and nothing in `helpdesk/` reaches for it — no pytz import anywhere in the library either, despite pytz being installed transitively.

**24 new tests**, three new modules: malformed MIME, hostile attachment filenames, POP3/IMAP socket timeouts mid-fetch (no real sockets, no sleeps), plus the first direct tests for `helpdesk/lib.py`.

**Ten pre-existing bugs pinned, not fixed** (all identical on both versions). The three worth flagging out loud, since each stops a whole queue: a `multipart/related` with no text part raises `AttributeError` at `email.py:899` (missing the None guard its sibling at `:885` has); an attachment named `../` lets `SuspiciousFileOperation` out of `process_as_attachment`; and a queue with `logging_type` + `logging_dir` set and no previous check hits a `TypeError` at `:104` and never recovers because the null default only exists in the other branch. Also: `sys.exit()` at four sites in `email.py` raises `SystemExit`, which `process_email`'s `except Exception` cannot catch, so a refused IMAP login kills every later queue — the existing `test_get_email_with_queue_failure` only raises a plain `Exception` and so passes while missing this. And `text_is_spam` is dead: `from akismet import Akismet` raises `ImportError` on the installed akismet 25.10.1 (which exports `SyncClient`/`Config` instead), so every public submission is waved through.

## 4. Left alone for other units

- **`requirements.txt:5`** (Unit 3): `akismet` pinned with no version, which is how the dead spam check happened. Whatever floor is chosen has to match the API `lib.py` gets rewritten against.
- **`helpdesk/models.py`** (Unit 8): read but not touched. `ensure_attachment_directory` and the `default_storage` check at `models.py:1189-1210` were already in place, so every attachment test ran green on 5.2 with no `DEFAULT_FILE_STORAGE` shim.

Full write-up with file:line, intended fixes and blast radius is in `docs/migration/reports/unit-7.md`.

Claude: Unit 7 verified, including the load-bearing check: reverting its `email.py` fix produces 4 errors on 4.2 and 2 on 5.2, exactly as reported. Its akismet finding is real too — the installed `akismet` 25.10.1 exports `SyncClient`/`Config`, not `Akismet`, so `helpdesk/lib.py:103` fails its import and the spam check is dead.

**Verified so far:**

| Unit | Tests (4.2 / 5.2) | Source change | Full suite |
|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | 72 OK / 72 OK | `public.py`, `kb.py`: `@login_not_required` on 6 bare public URLs | 382 OK / 3 known |
| 7 | 67 OK / 67 OK | `email.py`: guard `localtime()` against naive `now()` | 344 OK / 3 known |
| 9 | 95 OK / 95 OK | `forms.py`: pin `URLField.assume_scheme="http"` behind a version guard | 383 OK / 3 known |

Three units independently converged on the same root fact: Django 5.0 flipped the `USE_TZ` default to `True` and `quicktest.py` never sets it, so the two verification runs are not the same configuration. That is very likely the cause of Unit 6's three `test_api` failures. Waiting on Units 6 and 10.

Claude: The corrected regex preserves every current link decision. Updating the report.

Whole-suite regression: 4.2 `Ran 377 ... OK (skipped=5)`; 5.2 `FAILED (failures=3)`, the same three `test_api` failures (Unit 6's) that were already there at the branch point.

## 3. Headline findings

**No source change was forced.** I diffed every Django module these four files touch between the two installed trees and ran three probes under both venvs. The entire probe diff is four lines, none of which reaches this module:

- `user_passes_test` now sets `login_url`/`redirect_field_name` on the wrapper for `LoginRequiredMiddleware`. The anonymous redirect is byte-identical (`302 -> /login/?next=/tickets/1/`) on both.
- `formset_factory` stopped baking `get_default_renderer()` into the class and renamed it (`ChecklistTaskFormFormSet` -> `ChecklistTaskFormSet`). The instance resolves to the same renderer.
- Default form/formset `template_name` moved from `default.html` to `div.html`. The one formset here is rendered field-by-field via `task_form_row.html`, never as a bare `{{ formset }}` — this agrees with Unit 4's fix, and there was nothing left to re-fix.

`Paginator`, `Q`/`Case`/`When`, `JsonResponse`, `ContentType.get_for_model`, `requires_csrf_token` and `WSGIRequest` are unchanged for these call sites. `LoginRequiredMiddleware` would change nothing if a host enabled it: every staff view is meant to be behind a login, and `protect_view` (Unit 2) already opts the public views out.

So the deliverable is coverage: **60 new tests, 37 to 97**, plus **six pre-existing bugs found, pinned and reported** with verified fixes. Three are worth your attention:

- **`run_report` 500s on all three of its failure paths.** `get_report_queryset_or_redirect` returns 2-, 3- and 4-element tuples; the caller unpacks four. Reachable on a fresh install with no tickets, on any unknown report name (the URL pattern is `\w+`), and on an unloadable saved query.
- **"Accept and Close" neither closes nor accepts.** `?close` calls `update_ticket` with no `new_status`, so the ticket stays Resolved *and* its resolution gets overwritten with "Accepted resolution and closed ticket". I verified the one-line fix (`new_status=Ticket.CLOSED_STATUS`) fixes both halves.
- **`ticket_perm_check`'s second check is dead code.** It tests `can_access_queue` first and raises, so `can_access_ticket`'s assignee branch — which `test_ticket_actions.test_can_access_ticket` asserts works — cannot grant access through any view.

## 4. The two inherited items

- **Unit 4's unbound custom-fields form: not fixed, pinned.** Identical on both versions, so not migration-forced; the fix changes an observable outcome (a bad custom field would stop returning a successful redirect) and spans three files including Unit 4's template. Three tests pin it, and the report carries the intended fix.
- **Unit 1's two `num_to_link` quirks: not fixed, and `test_ticket_actions.test_num_to_link` left untouched.** The source is Unit 1's file, the output is version-independent, and Unit 1 already pinned both in `test_templatetags.py`. The report gives the exact regex (`(?<!&)#(\d+)\b` with `text[: match.start()]` and no added space) — I checked it against the current one on ten inputs, and it makes the same link decision every time, including `ticket#1` and the `&#1;` entity guard. My first proposed regex was wrong and dropped `ticket#1`; that is why I checked rather than shipping it. The report also gives the exact replacement assertions for `test_num_to_link:219-231`.

## 5. Left alone as another unit's

- `helpdesk/query.py:158-171` (Unit 6): `get_datatables_context` reads `kwargs.get("draw", [0])[0]` as if values were one-element lists, but its only caller (my `datatables_ticket_list`) passes `**request.query_params`, which unpacks to scalar strings — so `draw=12` becomes `1`. Same truncation on `length`, `start`, `search[value]` and both `order` params. Defaults save the no-parameter case, which is why nobody noticed. Fixing one side alone would break the other.
- `helpdesk/tests/test_time_spent.py:45` passes a naive `datetime.datetime.now()`, which now warns under 5.2's `USE_TZ=True` default. It is my module but existing test code, so I left it; the fix is `timezone.now()`.

One environment note for whoever runs the final integration: `quicktest.py` sets no `USE_TZ`, so 4.2 runs with `False` and 5.2 with `True` (Django 5.0 flipped the default). My timezone tests force `USE_TZ=True` so they mean the same thing on both.

## 3. Root cause of the three failures — and why I did not "fix" them

It is not a Django API change; it is a settings default. `django/conf/global_settings.py:44` is `USE_TZ = False` in the 4.2.30 tree and `USE_TZ = True` in the 5.2.17 tree, and `quicktest.py` never declares `USE_TZ`, so the harness silently changed configuration with the version. DRF's `rest_framework/fields.py:1186` (`return timezone.get_current_timezone() if settings.USE_TZ else None`) feeds `enforce_timezone`, and `django/forms/utils.py::from_current_timezone` does the same on the form side — but the decisive layer is `django/db/backends/sqlite3/operations.py:319` (`:315` on 4.2, byte-identical otherwise): `if settings.USE_TZ and not timezone.is_aware(value): value = timezone.make_aware(...)`. **Every** read of a `DateTimeField` comes back aware while `USE_TZ` is on, so `Ticket.objects.last().due_date` and `refresh_from_db()` are aware no matter what `helpdesk` does above them, and an aware datetime never equals the naive literal at `test_api.py:21`. I proved it is the setting rather than the version by running the suite with the flag forced both ways: **Django 4.2.30 with `USE_TZ=True` fails the same three and only those three; Django 5.2.17 with `USE_TZ=False` passes all 320.** So there is no Django 5.2 incompatibility anywhere in `helpdesk`.

The tests are what is wrong: `demo/…/settings.py:218` and `standalone/…/settings.py:224` both set `USE_TZ = True`, `helpdesk/settings.py:33` declares it (dead — nothing reads it), and `test_time_spent_auto.py:13` already carries `@override_settings(USE_TZ=True)`. The harness was the only place ever running tz-off, by accident. **Fix, needing `quicktest.py` which no unit owns:** add `USE_TZ=True,` next to `TIME_ZONE="UTC"`, and in the same commit update `test_api.py:21` to `datetime(2022, 4, 10, 15, 6, tzinfo=timezone.utc)` and `test_api.py:314` to `("date", "2022-06-30T23:09:44Z")`. `test_api.py:149`/`:188` then pass unchanged. The `USE_TZ=False` alternative also goes green with zero test edits but pins the suite to a configuration no install uses — recommend `True`. This is exactly the `pattern.md` step 8 case, and I checked that the fix does not belong in library code before escalating.

## 4. DRF

`.venv52` has **djangorestframework 3.18.0**. Read from its own installed metadata at `.venv52/lib/python3.11/site-packages/djangorestframework-3.18.0.dist-info/METADATA`: line 14 `Classifier: Framework :: Django :: 5.2`, line 30 `Requires-Dist: django>=5.2`. So 3.18.0 supports 5.2 and **cannot run on 4.2 at all**. `.venv` has 3.17.2, whose METADATA lines 14-18 classify 4.2/5.0/5.1/5.2/6.0 with `Requires-Dist: django>=4.2`.

For Unit 3: `requirements.txt` has no DRF floor at all. The evidenced floor covering both supported Django versions is `djangorestframework>=3.17.2`. Do **not** put `>=3.18` in `requirements.txt` — it makes a 4.2 install unsatisfiable. If DRF is pinned anywhere, `>=3.18` belongs in `constraints-Django5.txt` only. I edited nothing.

## 5. Left alone for other owners

- **`quicktest.py` — `USE_TZ`.** The blocker above.
- **`helpdesk/urls.py:189` (no unit owns this file).** `r"^rss/user/(?P<user_name>[^/]+)/"` has no `$` anchor, so it shadows `rss_user_queue` at `:194`. The per-queue RSS feed is unreachable: `reverse()` builds the URL, the link works, and it quietly serves the unfiltered feed for every queue; an unknown queue slug is a 200, not a 404. One-character fix. The Feed class itself is correct and is tested directly; both halves are pinned in `test_feeds.OpenTicketsByUserQueueTests`. Found while covering `feeds.py`, unrelated to the migration.
- **`helpdesk/settings.py:468-486` (shared, no owner).** `get_new_ticket_webhook_urls` splits on `","`; `get_followup_webhook_urls` uses `re.split(r"[\s],[\s]", …)`, which needs exactly one space each side of the comma, so `"http://a,http://b"` returns one malformed URL. Neither strips whitespace. Wants one shared splitter.
- **`helpdesk/views/api.py:39-41` and `:74-76`** — mine, not fixed on purpose. Both `get_queryset` methods iterate the entire queryset calling `set_custom_field_values()` (one query per `CustomField` per ticket, `models.py:923-929`) before the paginator slices, so listing page 1 of 25 loads every ticket in the DB. Pre-existing performance bug, not migration-forced; wants its own commit.
- **Pinned, not fixed** (all identical on both versions, each with a test naming the bug): `query.py:89` treats `query_params={}` as no params and raises `TypeError`; `query.py:39` splits on the literal `"OR"` so searching `FORM` becomes `F` OR `M`; `query.py:69`'s commented-out column 10 makes `order[0][column]=10` a `KeyError`/500; `query.py:136,139` mutate `self.params` so a second `get()` filters on nothing; `webhooks.py:34,60` catch `Timeout` only, so a refused connection turns ticket creation into a 500 and a 500 from the receiver is treated as delivered.
- **One test-isolation bug worth knowing about:** `test_webhooks.py:118-123` and `:228-231` set two `HELPDESK_*_WEBHOOK_URLS` env vars and never unset them, pointing at a server that is stopped when the test ends. Alphabetical discovery hides it in a full-suite run, but any module creating a ticket after `test_webhooks` in a named-module run errors on `ConnectionError`. I did not edit that existing test; `test_api_datetime_contract.py` neutralises the two getters in `setUp` so it is hermetic in any order.

One process note: the `scripts/test-touched.sh` PostToolUse hook reported a loader error on every edit. It runs `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` from the session cwd, which is the primary checkout, so it kept testing a tree where my new modules do not exist. Edits applied fine; I verified everything by running both venvs directly in the worktree. I did not touch `scripts/` or `.claude/`.

Claude: All five units are done, committed, and independently verified. Nothing merged.

## Verification (my own reruns, not the agents' claims)

| Unit | Branch | Tests 4.2 → 5.2 | Source change | Both green? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | `migrate/unit-5` | 10 → **72 / 72 OK** | `views/public.py`, `views/kb.py` | yes |
| 6 | `migrate/unit-6` | 22 → **108 OK / 108 FAILED (3)** | none | **no** |
| 7 | `migrate/unit-7` | 43 → **67 / 67 OK** | `email.py` | yes |
| 9 | `migrate/unit-9` | 32 → **95 / 95 OK** | `forms.py` | yes |
| 10 | `migrate/unit-10` | 37 → **97 / 97 OK** | none | yes |

Ownership audit is clean: no two branches touch the same file, and none touched `quicktest.py`, `models.py`, `migrations/`, the shared test helpers, or `scripts/`. No existing test assertion was weakened anywhere — the only deletions in test files are three import lines Unit 5 merged into longer ones.

## Unit 6 is red on 5.2, and it is right to be

It fixed nothing and reported instead, which the ground rules require when a fix would need `quicktest.py`. I tested its claim rather than taking it:

- Django **5.2 with `USE_TZ=False` → all 406 tests pass**
- Django **4.2 with `USE_TZ=True` → the same 3 `test_api` failures**

So the variable is the setting, not the version. `quicktest.py` never declares `USE_TZ`, and Django 5.0 flipped the global default from `False` to `True`, so the two verification commands have been running different configurations this whole time. Every read of a `DateTimeField` comes back aware under `USE_TZ=True`, and `test_api.py:21` compares against a naive literal. No library change can fix that.

I also verified its proposed fix: `USE_TZ=True` in `quicktest.py`, plus `test_api.py:21` made aware and `:314` given a `Z` suffix, takes the full suite to **406 OK on both 4.2.30 and 5.2.17**. That edit belongs to whoever owns integration — it touches an unowned file and an existing test.

Its DRF finding also checks out: `.venv` has 3.17.2 (`Requires-Dist: django>=4.2`), `.venv52` has 3.18.0 (`Requires-Dist: django>=5.2`). So `>=3.18` in `requirements.txt` would make a 4.2 install unsatisfiable; the honest floor is `djangorestframework>=3.17.2`, with `>=3.18` only in `constraints-Django5.txt`.

## Two other claims I spot-checked and confirmed

- Unit 7's `email.py` fix is load-bearing: reverting it gives 4 errors on 4.2 and 2 on 5.2, exactly as reported. Its akismet finding is real — installed `akismet` 25.10.1 exports `SyncClient`/`Config`, not `Akismet`, so `lib.py:103` raises `ImportError` and the spam check is dead.
- Unit 9's `URLField.assume_scheme` is genuinely new in 5.2 (`forms/fields.py:778`) and absent in 4.2, so the version guard is needed.

## Worth your attention before merging

Three units independently hit the same `USE_TZ` split, so decide that first — it changes what "green" means for every other unit. Beyond that, the reports carry a lot of pre-existing bugs found and pinned rather than fixed, several of which stop a whole queue or a whole page: Unit 10's `run_report` 500ing on all three failure paths and "Accept and Close" doing neither; Unit 7's `multipart/related` `AttributeError` and `sys.exit()` inside `process_email`; Unit 5's iframe spam-submission crash. None are migration-forced, all are pinned by tests, and each report gives the intended fix.

One process wart: `scripts/test-touched.sh` resolves the repo root from the session cwd, so for at least one agent it kept testing the primary checkout instead of its worktree. Edits still applied; I ran every verification myself in the correct worktree.
